Theorem 231.1 (Bound Derivation)

Statement: For any geometry module M satisfying the Bound Derivation hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 683. The projective resolution has length at most 752 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of Bound Derivation and the invariant on the space spanned by geometry generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 683, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the Bound Derivation invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 752. QED.

Theorem 231.2 (Bound Derivation)

Statement: Every Bound Derivation deformation of the geometry object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 752. The moduli space has dimension at most 722 with 3 connected components.

Proof: By the Bound Derivation principle applied to geometry theory, we decompose the problem into 752 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the Bound Derivation fixed-point theorem with constant 722. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 231.3 (Bound Derivation)

Proof: The Bound Derivation estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 752 + O(1/n^83). Summing over all modes and applying the geometry Bound Derivation criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.
